The concept wiki

Sources

Original talks and their concept-article mappings.

Coverage mappings validated against the supplied reader ledger.

"I've never seen anything scarier than an LLM with tool calls." — Erik Meijer aka @HeadinTheBox

Watch original · Original source report

  • Agent Authority, Isolation and Runtime Controls

    Own permissions, execution and emergency control; privacy retention and tenancy have their own article.

  • Prompt Injection and Agent Threat Modeling

    Explain attack paths and threat models; grant enforcement is treated in the access-control article.

  • Probing safety behavior and reward proxies

    Own behavior testing and scoring; application security exploitation is covered separately.

Limitations: Conceptual tutorial and strongly stated opinion; no quantitative attack evaluation, complete executable harness, or deployment evidence is provided.; The slides contain type signatures, code, interpreter, and proof sketches that cannot be reconstructed completely from the transcript.; Claims of mathematically proven safety are restricted by what a program language, interpreter, and formal policy actually model; the talk itself rejects a general formal definition of a safe natural-language answer.; A file-deletion anecdote is not a controlled incident investigation; broad claims that models are intrinsically dangerous are the speaker’s opinion.; Timestamped captions are retained, but some names and technical terms are corrupted; no substantive Q&A follows the ending.

"My name is... my name is...": A Linguistic Map for Voice Agents — Midam Kim, ServiceNow

Watch original · Original source report

  • Monitoring production quality and improvement

    Operational measurement and uncertainty, not human decision rights. Owns quality signals, test evolution, risk and task outcomes; execution traces and platform plumbing are covered elsewhere. Observability, discovery and continuous evaluation rather than offline release authorization.

  • Voice interaction and speech system evaluation

    Covers voice interaction and speech system evaluation; other articles own their distinct mechanisms and evaluation targets.

Limitations: This is a conceptual linguistic framework supported by an illustrative personal experience, not a controlled evaluation or quantified improvement study.; Eva bench is named as an end-to-end diagnostic benchmark, but its tasks, metrics, protocol and results are not described.; Captions garble some speech-recognition acronyms and the precise name-pronunciation discussion; only the general failure mechanics are clear.; No implementation, dynamic-orchestration algorithm or separate Q&A is present.

"The biggest challenge in your stack? Evals, Evals, Evals" - 2026 State of AI Engineering results

Watch original · Original source report

  • Agent Authority, Isolation and Runtime Controls

    Own permissions, execution and emergency control; privacy retention and tenancy have their own article.

  • Choosing agent delegation and coordination

    Own topology, specialist coordination and capacity tradeoffs; link model portfolio selection to routing-and-deployment.

  • Comparing task quality, cost and value

    Covers outcome economics and controlled comparisons, not inference-server tuning. Accounting and value proxies, not inference-server configuration.

  • Allocating models and deployment resources

    Covers task routing, provider selection and specialization rather than low-level serving internals. Application model quality and choices, rather than hardware-level serving metrics.

  • Synthetic respondents and historical personas

    Covers synthetic respondents and historical personas; other articles own their distinct mechanisms and evaluation targets.

  • Tracing, failure analysis and evaluator methods

    Owns trace evidence, diagnostic scope and evaluator methods; execution environment design and statistical comparison are separate.

  • What evaluation can establish: design, evidence and uncertainty

    Teaches evaluation structure, evidence scope, uncertainty in claims and evaluation maturity; specific experimental estimators and product economics belong elsewhere. Owns control, sampling, uncertainty and causal interpretation; does not teach item-response models.

Limitations: Self-selected or otherwise unspecified sampling and builder-heavy respondents limit generalization; the transcript does not give full methodology or uncertainty intervals.; Year-to-year differences need not represent change in the same organizations.

"The engineer of the future is the person who chooses what is worth doing." — Addy Osmani, Anthropic

Watch original · Original source report

  • Choosing agent delegation and coordination

    Own topology, specialist coordination and capacity tradeoffs; link model portfolio selection to routing-and-deployment.

  • Comparing agents without confounding the result

    Covers treatment identity and execution integrity, not evaluation task meaning.

  • Delivering agent-assisted work with human review

    Owns software-delivery practices and adoption; runtime controls and evaluation infrastructure are separate. Review capacity, decisions and handoffs rather than test-suite construction.

  • Tracing, failure analysis and evaluator methods

    Owns trace evidence, diagnostic scope and evaluator methods; execution environment design and statistical comparison are separate.

Limitations: The publisher marks this first-party transcript needs_review, and it retains disfluencies and occasional transcription artifacts.; Survey percentages and research findings are secondhand; underlying methods, effect sizes, and source documents are not supplied.; Primarily conceptual engineering and career guidance; no implemented control system or measured before-and-after result is demonstrated.

1 Trillion Phone Calls/yr, 10% Error rate: The Crisis in Voice AI — Sumanyu Sharma, Hamming AI

Watch original · Original source report

  • Evaluation-driven improvement

    Covers experiment design and regression evidence, not the optimizer’s search algorithm.

  • Generate synthetic cases without mistaking them for reality

    Recipes, checks and sampling limitations; interactive simulators are treated separately.

  • Learning from production evidence and regression tests

    Improvement processes and feedback, not raw execution controls. Behavioral suites and predeployment evidence rather than continuous production monitoring. Owns task verification, comparisons and replay; incident management is separate.

  • Observability, Monitoring and Audit Integrity

    Own live detection and audit integrity; sensitive trace retention belongs in privacy.

  • Production governance, readiness and release

    Release and accountability decisions, not detailed security threat controls. Owns readiness, explicit promotion criteria, judge stability and recovery checks; live monitoring is elsewhere. Owns exposure, accountability and runtime safeguards; evaluation methods are separate.

  • Prompt Injection and Agent Threat Modeling

    Explain attack paths and threat models; grant enforcement is treated in the access-control article.

  • Matching evidence and autonomy to risk

    Focus on system risk, harm prioritization and deployment decisions; detailed technical enforcement belongs in agent controls.

  • Probing safety behavior and reward proxies

    Own behavior testing and scoring; application security exploitation is covered separately.

  • Voice interaction and speech system evaluation

    Covers voice interaction and speech system evaluation; other articles own their distinct mechanisms and evaluation targets.

Limitations: The claimed approximately ten-percent error rate across ten thousand monitored agents has no disclosed error definition, sampling procedure or domain breakdown.; The roughly one-in-five red-team break rate is vendor-reported without protocol, attack budgets or a representative sample.; The medical booking is a personal anecdote; allergy and credit-card examples illustrate consequences rather than measured incident rates.; Trillion-call scale and future adoption are speaker estimates/projections. Captions garble some proper names and security references. No separate Q&A is present.

1,000 Agent Tasks in a Sandbox: What Breaks When LLMs Run Code — Kevin Orellana, AWS

Watch original · Original source report

  • Tracing and diagnosing production behavior

    Evidence capture and root-cause investigation, not a proof of user-visible success. Owns trace collection, privacy, triage, attribution and cost; it does not itself decide release readiness. Owns live discovery and incident handling; release gates and memory maintenance are separate.

  • Operating secure, governed agent environments

    Security-specific threats and controls, distinct from general release decisions. Workload execution, access and infrastructure testing rather than semantic product quality.

Limitations: The suite size was explicitly arbitrary and exploratory. The full test corpus, result database, failure counts, test oracle quality and run costs are not supplied.; No controlled attribution study or statistically representative customer-workload evaluation is presented. Generalization beyond the vendor’s sandbox and applications is limited.; Captions ambiguously render a null-byte-related failure and several kernel/security terms. Do not derive authoritative low-level definitions or exact mechanisms from those passages.; External benchmark-exploit and broken-tool studies are alluded to without enough detail to independently verify their numerical claims here. No substantive Q&A appears.

1,368 AI Failures and What They Teach About Evals — Vishal Srivastava, New York Life & Tanmay Sah

Watch original · Original source report

  • Agreement, truth and the limits of evaluator proxies

    Epistemic and construct-validity limits, not the operational labeling procedure.

  • Document extraction and legal review

    Covers document extraction and legal review; other articles own their distinct mechanisms and evaluation targets.

  • Improvement loops, adaptation and overfitting

    Owns optimization loops and adaptation tradeoffs; empirical comparison design and memory architecture are separate.

  • Production governance, readiness and release

    Release and accountability decisions, not detailed security threat controls. Owns readiness, explicit promotion criteria, judge stability and recovery checks; live monitoring is elsewhere. Owns exposure, accountability and runtime safeguards; evaluation methods are separate.

  • Matching evidence and autonomy to risk

    Focus on system risk, harm prioritization and deployment decisions; detailed technical enforcement belongs in agent controls.

  • Verifying agent outcomes and diagnosing behavior

    Covers score surfaces and diagnostic traces, not verifier security or environment fidelity.

Limitations: Title says 1,368 failures; the spoken transcript does not document inclusion criteria, labels, inter-rater reliability, denominators, or statistical methods.; Claims of almost 98% coverage, six dimensions per incident, and stronger deployment-control harm associations are speaker-reported retrospective findings, not causal proof or independently verified forecasts.; The materiality scale is described without scoring details, thresholds, or validation sufficient for reproduction.; The airline remediation is proposed counterfactually, not tested in the reported incident.; The confirmation-bias experiment is retold imprecisely; no source is supplied. Captions have disfluencies and lack segment timestamps; Q&A is offered later but not transcribed.

20 days of compute vs 7 hours: rethinking what state-of-the-art means — Bertrand Charpentier, Pruna

Watch original · Original source report

  • Benchmarks, leaderboards and model selection

    Owns benchmark transfer, rank interpretation, lifecycle and local selection; does not replace measurement of deployed-system reliability.

  • Comparing task quality, cost and value

    Covers outcome economics and controlled comparisons, not inference-server tuning. Accounting and value proxies, not inference-server configuration.

  • Design evaluation distributions for decisions

    Coverage and estimands rather than individual-label accuracy.

  • Budgeting evaluation and monitoring

    Covers evaluation feedback and monitor budgets rather than product-task quality itself. Evaluation, verification and monitoring economics, not serving configuration.

  • From business problem to meaningful success criteria

    Owns problem framing, construct selection and customer interpretation; detailed experiment design and model comparisons are separate.

  • Model selection and runtime efficiency

    Covers runtime selection and acceleration, not training model specialization.

  • Validate judges against human labels

    Alignment and classifier errors, not truth or probability calibration.

Limitations: Image comparisons, leaderboard tables, metric plots, and Pareto frontiers are not fully available as data in the transcript.; Energy, marathon analogies, latency, and cost numbers are speaker-reported and include captioned inconsistencies; no independent energy or hardware protocol is given.; The speaker promotes models and compression tools from his company, so the efficiency comparison should remain a vendor case rather than a neutral ranking.; Claims about statistical significance are not accompanied by confidence intervals, power analysis, or precise application-distribution definitions.; The Q&A answer is present through EOF, but the actual audience question is not transcribed and the final line ends informally.
